## 5. score 54 · chat · Playdead Unofficial - ARG - solving [461275582970462209].txt:214512

finding something in the game, rearranging strings to form an image, making squares from strings, getting Game of Life pattern, overlaying pixels, Braille, Morse code, disconnecting Switch controllers, Polybius cipher, overlaying strings onto poem, pressing a printer button on the website, finding something on Google, going to websites on terminal41, binary code, turning colors into binary, taking alpha channel of image, turning image of waveform into sound, isolating specific colors in image, overlaying images, reversing SHA-256 hashes, converting string into BMP, collecting stickers, putting together sticker backgrounds into an image

Context:

Interestingly, most of the puzzles so far have been much less technical actually. The only &quot;cipher&quot; was the Polybius cipher (turning 14-42-54-55 into a 5x5 square of letters), and the most technical puzzle was the :acorn42: BMP (because we had to fix the encoding from UTF-8 to ANSI, and replace space with NUL characters). I looked through the ARG and these are all the solved puzzle types and steps so far: | | finding something in the game, rearranging strings to form an image, making squares from strings, getting Game of Life pattern, overlaying pixels, Braille, Morse code, disconnecting Switch controllers, Polybius cipher, overlaying strings onto poem, pressing a printer button on the website, finding something on Google, going to websites on terminal41, binary code, turning colors into binary, taking alpha channel of image, turning image of waveform into sound, isolating specific colors in image, overlaying images, reversing SHA-256 hashes, converting string into BMP, collecting stickers, putting together sticker backgrounds into an image

We suspect that the stickers repeat in a 108 pattern, but 108 is not the only number that can mathematically happen. These are the possible cycle lengths, the list starts with 108, the most probable. I always run the numbers when a new sticker appears, and this is the first time this year that this list became shorter. We now know for sure that the cycle is not 256, 294, 353, 358, or 385.

## 53. score 36 · chat · Playdead Unofficial - ARG - solving [461275582970462209].txt:213096

Groups: sticker_core,period_prediction

The probability that we find a sticker in the 001 to 478 range is 478/597 and so the probability that **all** 70 stickers are in this range is just (478/597)^70, which equates to 0.000017%. So it is extremely unlikely that all 70 of our stickers would lie in the 001 to 478 range.

Context:

If we assume there are 597 stickers, one for each number from 001 to 597, what is the probability that after finding 70 stickers, none of them will be in the 479 to 596 range? | | The probability that we find a sticker in the 001 to 478 range is 478/597 and so the probability that **all** 70 stickers are in this range is just (478/597)^70, which equates to 0.000017%. So it is extremely unlikely that all 70 of our stickers would lie in the 001 to 478 range.

## 54. score 36 · chat · Playdead Unofficial - General - inside [461275526716194818].txt:149695

Groups: sticker_core

They are harder to find because they first started to be received in around december 2019, kickstarting the ARG over here. Then several of us pitched in and searched for owners of the collector&#x27;s edition, and we have gotten 70 confirmed sticker images. 33 lost stickers and 21 unknown stickers. So a total of 124 owners found if I got that count right. There&#x27;s estimated to be 500 or 600 collector&#x27;s editions because iam8bit has number 597, the highest number, no one else has 500 or above. And finding new owners is incredibly hard as not even close to all of the owners post it for various reasons I guess. I and several others have been searching high and low over all kinds of social media and internet with all possibilities of keywords etc to yield results... Ebay had some for sale for example, but are now sold, or just the same ones... Also the collector&#x27;s edition is expensive, 400$ with shipping, maybe up to 500$ including taxes and customs depending on where you live

## 55. score 35 · chat · Playdead Unofficial - ARG - tldr [462637922944811028].txt:533

Groups: sticker_core,symbols_codes,external_consumers,physical_material

The stickers in the Collector&#x27;s Edition with a morse/number had some kind of relief with dots and symbols printed. Taking high resolution images from the stickers, and looking at their numbers, we found that they could be categorized into nine groups (A, B, C, D, E, F, G, H, I) where each contained the same hidden symbols, but could contain different main character (/, - and •). Gathering a picture of a sticker from each category and a bit of image processing gave as a result the image attached. It&#x27;s a picture of another perspective of the initial morse printer, with the main characters and underlying symbols

Interesting thing is Sudoku also consists of 27 total housings, as in rows, where there are 9 horizontal lines of cells. And columns where there are 9 vertical lines of cells. Last being boxes (or Blocks): The 9 distinct 3×3 square grids. If you add 27 onto 81, you get 108. Maybe someone in here is good with numbers, Sudoku, and potentially has ideas of what can be tested🤔

Context:

Also it’s 1 big square divided into 9 big main squares, each containing 9 squares inside of it as in 3x3. The most intuitive explanation to this is the same as the printer puzzle: perhaps the stickers are rotating, with a code repeating every 15 or 20 stickers or something along those lines? This would be relatively simple to check by lining up the different stickers we&#x27;ve found and seeing if there&#x27;s any lengths that their codes seem repeat exceptionally much at (of course they have made a couple mistakes in the past so we would have to do some thorough checking to make sure there&#x27;s not just one-character different or something like that)

Context:

Before making any guesses, we have to consider something first. Clearly playdead can&#x27;t expect us to have EVERY SINGLE sticker in the entire list to be able to solve the code, that would be unreasonable of them (and their already-proven lead in the stickers has shown us to only in theory needed to collect nine total if we got very lucky) so whatever code it is must be concluded from only a small handful of semi-random stickers. | | The most intuitive explanation to this is the same as the printer puzzle: perhaps the stickers are rotating, with a code repeating every 15 or 20 stickers or something along those lines? ## 82. score 27 · chat · Playdead Unofficial - General - inside [461275526716194818].txt:117925

Groups: sticker_core,external_consumers,physical_material,period_prediction

Basically we found that the stickers have a pattern in the background image that gives one of nine total repeating patterns. We named those A, B, C, D, E, F, G, H, I. Each one resembles one of 9 total images, that together became 1 full image. The other thing is that each background image had letters/text hid in it, and by combining what we got in the full image which was shown to be the printer close to the Huddle in-game, it gave us an unique code

## 124. score 23 · chat · Playdead Unofficial - ARG - solving [461275582970462209].txt:176342

Groups: registration_geometry,symbols_codes,external_consumers,physical_material

From what I&#x27;ve seen of the solved puzzles and what the amazingly hard working code crackers here have done, each encoding has been unique some from text in-game, some from the morse printer, some from the website with the printer icon. So the only common thread to possibly find would be to identify what sphere of encoding has sparked Playdead&#x27;s interests and what similar methods would likely be in use. There&#x27;s some margin of error, because all the puzzles haven&#x27;t been solved. One mistake in processing a piece can throw off the whole message as well so even being on the right track can lead to a dead end.

Context:

There are a lot of different ways of hiding information. The the first split happening at physical or digital, digital encoding can be anything from an image needing certain portions added or eliminated, singular colours, specific patterns, then there&#x27;s contrast to find things out of place that blend well, then there&#x27;s audio that can be hidden in the image of a soundwave, audio clips forming an image, text that can be put through hex, binary and morse among other unconventional languages. | The second path on that is pen-to-paper codes, this includes field ciphers used by the military to encode messages, working on the principal that if you have a keyword you can decode a message from text using a simple grid of letters, but the list goes on when it comes to decoding text on paper, the variations on the norm alone can make a big difference, transposition, disrupted transposition, fractionation, the list goes on. | From what I&#x27;ve seen of the solved puzzles and what the amazingly hard working code crackers here have done, each encoding has been unique some from text in-game, some from the morse printer, some from the website with the printer icon. So the only common thread to possibly find would be to identify what sphere of encoding has sparked Playdead&#x27;s interests and what similar methods would likely be in use. There&#x27;s some margin of error, because all the puzzles haven&#x27;t been solved. One mistake in processing a piece can throw off the whole message as well so even being on the right track can lead to a dead end.

Groups: sticker_core,external_consumers,physical_material

Early on the printer was just a static asset to be used as background noise. Routinely as production drew to a conclusion the content creators on the team got more and more time on their hands. So along with general polish they also had the time and capability to put in small homages, secrets and Easter Eggs here and there until content lock down was imposed. Even after the lock down, the printer was still allowed to be fiddled with. Now that there was a secret there, it also had to work and have something nice for the audience to discover at the end of it. &quot;

Context:

&quot;Over the course of a couple of years detectives have been stabbing at the printer secret ever since they first discovered its existence. Vital to the developer is the ability to follow the process of the solving party – however Playdead don’t know much about the inner workings and discussions of the solving party, but they have systems in place where the solving party communicates indirectly to them, and gives them hints at how far they’ve reached. | | Early on the printer was just a static asset to be used as background noise. Routinely as production drew to a conclusion the content creators on the team got more and more time on their hands. ## 212. score 19 · chat · Playdead Unofficial - ARG - solving-breakout [463106924708233216].txt:9934

Groups: external_consumers,physical_material

`2016:08:09 14:12:24` was time, when image was created. There also `2016-12-16T14:12:24.351` - time, when image was last edited. There also name of author: `AnSet`. I suppose it&#x27;s short for Anthony Setrinamairé. This is part of EXIF metadata, stored as TIFF format, starts at marker `FF E1`. `&amp;&#x27;()*56789:CDEFGHIJSTUVWXYZcdefghijstuvwxyz` is part of Huffman codes tree. There two of it, because Jpeg uses two tables - one for brightness (Y channel) and one for colors (Cb and Cr). In our case they are common ones. Data got corrupted before we got it, all symbols between hex `80` and `FF` (128 and 255) changed to `EF BF BD`, and all `00` turned to `20`, but it&#x27;s easy to restore by copying from other image. There also some other data, as size of image and jpeg specification. By the way - size of image is somewhere between 128x128 and 255x255. Part starts at `FF C0`. Between these parts also quantization tables, in our case it standard one by number of 79, all but last 20 must be turned to 00.

Context:

| [12/20/2025 4:11 PM] eropkol | `2016:08:09 14:12:24` was time, when image was created. There also `2016-12-16T14:12:24.351` - time, when image was last edited. There also name of author: `AnSet`. I suppose it&#x27;s short for Anthony Setrinamairé. This is part of EXIF metadata, stored as TIFF format, starts at marker `FF E1`. `&amp;&#x27;()*56789:CDEFGHIJSTUVWXYZcdefghijstuvwxyz` is part of Huffman codes tree. There two of it, because Jpeg uses two tables - one for brightness (Y channel) and one for colors (Cb and Cr). In our case they are common ones. Data got corrupted before we got it, all symbols between hex `80` and `FF` (128 and 255) changed to `EF BF BD`, and all `00` turned to `20`, but it&#x27;s easy to restore by copying from other image. There also some other data, as size of image and jpeg specification. By the way - size of image is somewhere between 128x128 and 255x255. Part starts at `FF C0`. Between these parts also quantization tables, in our case it standard one by number of 79, all but last 20 must be turned to 00.

## 297. score 13 · chat · Playdead Unofficial - ARG - solving-breakout [463106924708233216].txt:10754

Groups: registration_geometry,symbols_codes,period_prediction

But, since most rows have only 1 or 2 symbols missing, we can look at the usually 2 or 4 possibilities of letters we would get from this shifting method, and see if we can form any english words. For example, if grey=0 and red=1, then the first row `0_01` is either 1 or 5 in binary, which shifts `F` to one of `{g, k}` forwards, or `{e, a}` backwards. Or, if grey=1 and red=0, then we get `{t, p}` or `{r, v}`. So from `Fordsjh`, we could get `kyundor` for example in the first case, or `agothex` in the second case. I don&#x27;t see any meaningful words we could get in either of the 4 cases, so this is probably not it.

Context:

Or, if grey=1 and red=0, then we get `{t, p}` or `{r, v}`. So from `Fordsjh`, we could get `kyundor` for example in the first case, or `agothex` in the second case. What if the worms that controlled the pig were not made by the scientists - but are actual extraterrestrial lifeforms that were taken from the newly discovered planet and brought back to Earth for analysis? And the research from said analysis enabled these new rapid technologies in regards to creating these artificial lifeforms. This would explain the outdated technology. It&#x27;s not that they&#x27;re using &quot;outdated technology&quot;, it&#x27;s just that Inside takes place in the past, in a universe where humans stumbled across aliens and with it technologies that in retrospect they maybe just weren&#x27;t fully prepared for.

Context:

The scientists in Inside are using quite outdated technology. They seem to be super advanced in some fields (they&#x27;re lab growing their own humans!), but seem to still be relying on old computers and tapes for storage. It&#x27;s safe to assume that they did not make it to another planet through extended space travel, no way. | But what if, in actuality, they found a way to travel to this new planet by other means? Could be anything, really. Maybe a wormhole in our solar system (interstellar anyone), heck maybe even some sort of transdimensinoal portal right here on earth, artificial or not. What&#x27;s important is, this actually opens up a lot of theories. | What if the worms that controlled the pig were not made by the scientists - but are actual extraterrestrial lifeforms that were taken from the newly discovered planet and brought back to Earth for analysis? ## 323. score 12 · chat · Playdead Unofficial - ARG - tldr [462637922944811028].txt:227

Groups: symbols_codes,external_consumers,physical_material,period_prediction

We seemingly figured out the Switch puzzle. After waiting until the morse message &quot;CTRL CON DISCON&quot; finishes and the printer goes back to printing only grey pages, you then have to disconnect and connect your left and right controllers in the particular sequence RRLRLL. As you&#x27;re doing this you will get responses from the printer indicating that you are following the correct sequence (consecutive white pages) or that you broke the sequence and you have to start again (black page). Once you finish this, the printer will then print more morse, which appears to be a string of two codes, each corresponding to the color of the controllers you have. Entering all these codes into the playdead website caused more printreqstatus pages to appear. At this point the switch puzzle seems nearly solved, but it seems to be suggesting that we have to solve the other two puzzles first.

Context:

| [7/7/2018 8:50 AM] Deleted User | We seemingly figured out the Switch puzzle. After waiting until the morse message &quot;CTRL CON DISCON&quot; finishes and the printer goes back to printing only grey pages, you then have to disconnect and connect your left and right controllers in the particular sequence RRLRLL. As you&#x27;re doing this you will get responses from the printer indicating that you are following the correct sequence (consecutive white pages) or that you broke the sequence and you have to start again (black page). Once you finish this, the printer will then print more morse, which appears to be a string of two codes, each corresponding to the color of the controllers you have. Entering all these codes into the playdead website caused more printreqstatus pages to appear. At this point the switch puzzle seems nearly solved, but it seems to be suggesting that we have to solve the other two puzzles first.

## 342. score 12 · chat · Playdead Unofficial - General - inside [461275526716194818].txt:167684

Ye in that post they referred to the Xbox 360 version of LIMBO which was the first and at that time the only version out there, which was an exclusive version. Didn&#x27;t have the same dark route as other versions, it had a closed off version, different routing, and you&#x27;d have to return back to the surface. But it did have the eggs that the other versions had. But it also has 11 extra eggs, which are exclusive to Xbox 360, so 21 in total. Anyways, each of those additional eggs would give 1% extra completion on the leaderboard (no achievement), so the max you could get is 111% with all of them found and all other achievements. The hardest egg being that you complete the game without dying, and only then that egg would fall down from the skies after breaking through the glass at the end

## 401. score 10 · chat · Playdead Unofficial - ARG - solving-breakout [463106924708233216].txt:10529

Groups: external_consumers,physical_material,period_prediction

It&#x27;s very likely a coincidence. In printer manual letters repeats several times because it&#x27;s test for how letters will look like on paper. In jpeg code text repeats 2 times because it&#x27;s two Huffman tables, one for brightness and one for two other color related channels, and letters there is coincidence too, it have all hex codes from 09 to FA, and all english letters are hex codes 41 to 5A for uppercase and 61 to 7A for lowercase.

Context:

| [12/24/2025 7:56 PM] eropkol | It&#x27;s very likely a coincidence. In printer manual letters repeats several times because it&#x27;s test for how letters will look like on paper. In jpeg code text repeats 2 times because it&#x27;s two Huffman tables, one for brightness and one for two other color related channels, and letters there is coincidence too, it have all hex codes from 09 to FA, and all english letters are hex codes 41 to 5A for uppercase and 61 to 7A for lowercase.

## 438. score 10 · chat · Playdead Unofficial - General - inside [461275526716194818].txt:181918

Groups: sticker_core,physical_material

its funny how they paid attention to detail on everything except pacing. It&#x27;s almost as if it was intentional. At the start of the game, before going over the wall by climbing the fridge you can watch over the guys in the background, they do absolutely nothing, only the dog moves. Just when you got off the wall then the truck starts moving. Next, right before a car is chasing the boy, just right before he walked away from the trees, theres the cars sound, however it doesn&#x27;t get louder or quieter. Finally, the people looking down at the huddle in the cafetaria and the people following in the background. The people in the background follows the huddle, but they stop so abruptly when they&#x27;re done walking, the people at the second floor of the cafetaria doesn&#x27;t even look at the huddle, just kind of there. It was what set me off the most when I played the game, I was expecting them to atleast run to the other side to look at the thing, but no they were just kind of there.
